# Corn Oil

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="corn-oil-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="corn-oil-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="corn-oil-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Corn Oil in the HMI library has 99 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, tin, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, V, As and Ba from 11 sources.<sup </li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Corn Oil, oil from Traditional corn oil had lead higher at 32.4 µg/kg, against 19.3 µg/kg for Commercial corn oil in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Tayeb J, Movassaghghazani M · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd">Assessment of lead and cadmium exposure through olive and corn oil consumption in Gonbad-Kavus, north of Iran: A public health risk analysis</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxicology Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" Across 5 other studies of ordinary farms and markets, reported lead means and medians on an as-sold or fresh-weight basis ranged from 3.2 µg/kg (S-T et al. 2024) to 86.8 µg/kg (authors 2009).<sup class="cite">[[sources/authors2009-cd-cr-cu-mn-y-pb-en-diferentes|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Unknown authors · 2009</span><a et al. 2024) to 12.3 µg/kg (authors 2009).<sup class="cite">[[sources/authors2009-cd-cr-cu-mn-y-pb-en-diferentes|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Unknown authors · 2009</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/authors2009-cd-cr-cu-mn-y-pb-en-diferentes">(Cd, Cr, Cu, Mn, y Pb) en diferentes tipos de aceites class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Lin S-T, Liao K-W, Xuan T-C, Chiou T-Y, Lin Z-E, Lee W-J · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/lin2024-edible-oils-taiwan-12-metals">Determination, distribution, and health risk assessment of 12 heavy metals in various edible oils in Taiwan</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">JSFA Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/lin2024-edible-oils-taiwan-12-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Higher results came from contaminated or exposed settings rather than ordinary supply. Corn Oil, oil from Traditional corn oil had lead higher at 32.4 µg/kg, against 19.3 µg/kg for Commercial corn oil in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Tayeb J, Movassaghghazani M · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd">Assessment of lead and cadmium exposure through olive and corn oil consumption in Gonbad-Kavus, north of Iran: A public health risk analysis</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxicology Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the corn Oil came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as corn Oil

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-corn-oil" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-corn-oil-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of corn oil, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤30</td><td>≤69</td><td>≤98</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤30</td><td>≤69</td><td>≤98</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>13 reference units; 2 sampling families. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>12</td><td>50%</td></tr><tr><td>Ashraf 2012</td><td>1</td><td>50%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for corn oil, oil on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>2 sampling families with different designs; none is a probability sample of world supply. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li><li><a href="../sources/ashraf2012-heavy-metals-vegetable-oils-saudi-arabia" class="internal" data-slug="sources/ashraf2012-heavy-metals-vegetable-oils-saudi-arabia" data-no-cite-meta>Ashraf 2012</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>hmi-pooled-reference-v2:corn-oil-21b49493c8de:171855c50ece</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach corn Oil

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

The evidence on how metals reach corn Oil comes from studies that compared exposed and ordinary settings. Corn Oil, oil from Traditional corn oil had lead higher at 32.4 µg/kg, against 19.3 µg/kg for Commercial corn oil in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Tayeb J, Movassaghghazani M · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd">Assessment of lead and cadmium exposure through olive and corn oil consumption in Gonbad-Kavus, north of Iran: A public health risk analysis</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxicology Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

These are observational comparisons from single studies; the size of the difference depends on the site, metal and distance.

(upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · <a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for corn Oil

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Avoiding exposed growing areas lowered the metal measured in the studies available, but no study measured the cost or supply effect of doing so.<sup class="cite">[[sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Tayeb J, Movassaghghazani M · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd">Assessment of lead and cadmium exposure through olive and corn oil consumption in Gonbad-Kavus, north of Iran: A public health risk analysis</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxicology Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing corn Oil for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 0.85 µg/kg (authors 2009) to 100 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup <a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Corn Oil FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-corn-oil" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-corn-oil" data-question="What heavy metals are found in corn Oil?">

Local Population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Asian Journal of Chemistry (Uncorrected Proof)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/ashraf2012-heavy-metals-vegetable-oils-saudi-arabia">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/authors2009-cd-cr-cu-mn-y-pb-en-diferentes|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Unknown authors · 2009</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/authors2009-cd-cr-cu-mn-y-pb-en-diferentes">(Cd, Cr, Cu, Mn, y Pb) en diferentes tipos de aceites vegeta- high levels of lead.</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Peer-reviewed article</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/authors2009-cd-cr-cu-mn-y-pb-en-diferentes">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in corn Oil, oil from 15 results in 1 reporting region, with subgroup medians below 40 µg/kg, mostly non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span <details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-do-heavy-metals-get-into-corn-oil" data-anchor="how-do-heavy-metals-get-into-corn-oil" data-question="How do heavy metals get into corn Oil?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How do heavy metals get into corn Oil?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

In the studies available, corn Oil from contaminated or exposed settings carried more metal than corn Oil from the same study’s control.<sup class="cite">[[sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Tayeb J, Movassaghghazani M · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd">Assessment of lead and cadmium exposure through olive and corn oil consumption in Gonbad-Kavus, north of Iran: A public health risk analysis</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxicology Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Corn Oil, oil from Traditional corn oil had lead higher at 32.4 µg/kg, against 19.3 µg/kg for Commercial corn oil in the same study.<sup class="cite">[[sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd|7]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Tayeb J, Movassaghghazani M · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd">Assessment of lead and cadmium exposure through olive and corn oil consumption in Gonbad-Kavus, north of Iran: A public health risk analysis</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxicology Reports</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/tayeb2025-olive-corn-oil-iran-pb-cd">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-corn-oil-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-corn-oil-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in corn Oil is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in corn Oil is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 2 results from 2 sources, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|8]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) 2014 reported inorganic arsenic at 14.9 µg/kg (mean) in corn Oil, oil from European dietary-survey occurrence data.<sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|8]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
